## 1. Extraction: every numeral the block quotes (block lines 21-133)

Rule. block lines 21-133 read at the record pin; a numeral token is a maximal digit run with internal '.' or ',' groups (regex \d+(?:[.,]\d+)*|\.\d+) or a number word (zero..twelve, half; case-insensitive, word-bounded). Each token is classified in this order: inside a hand-mapped ledger row's span -> that row; inside a hand-declared exclusion span -> its category; else a generic rule (backticks, date, hash, arXiv/figure locator, file name or path, section sign, digits attached to a name, link/card/tier/stage/level/item/version prefix, one-/two-sided); else UNASSIGNED.

Count: **506 numeral tokens** in block lines 21-133 (471 digit tokens, 35 number words). Classified:

| class | tokens |
|---|---|
| excluded (generic) | 211 |
| excluded (hand) | 39 |
| row (identifier inside a row span) | 20 |
| row (note-figure numeral) | 236 |

Ledger rows: 166 (hand-mapped, each a span of one block line). Hand exclusion spans: 31. Unassigned: **0**.

Numeral-free rows. Every numeral token above is assigned by the rule. A span without a numeral gets a row by this rule: a numeral-free span gets a row when the block lists it among the figures to be reproduced for the note: an entry of an item's figure list, a qualifier or definition attached to quoted figures, an N3(ii)-(v) result, or a figure label; requests for an account, a method, a provenance or a table get none (examples in numeral_free_no_row: Code's read of block lines 21-133, not an exhaustive enumeration). Examples with rows: 'zero pauses' (L101), 'the three conjuncts' (L108), 'the leakage gate's bound' (L146), F1's link-5 y-axis label (L081), and, added in round 2 after the values check, 'the random-direction comparison' (L162, :43), N3(v)'s random-direction control (L163, :48), the definition of Δ (L164, :57), 'the falsifier' (L165, :104) and 'the purity–potency figures' (L166, :107). Rows L001-L161 keep their round-1 ids; L162-L166 sit in block-line order among them.

'Identifier inside a row span' counts digits that sit inside a row's span but are names (L60, random-1, F1, link-5, Horn-1); they belong to the row's wording, not its value, and --note matching ignores them. A decimal after 'word-' is a quantity, not a name (round 2: 'sub-0.05-cosine' in L161 counts as a figure).

## 2. The ledger

One row per figure as the block quotes it. 'item raw row' is the item raw's own line at the audit pin. 'printed', 'source' and 'status' are the item raw's, verbatim (long values are cut at 400 characters here; the JSON carries them whole). 'entry (record)' = the latest commit, over the row's record sources, at which a source entered the record (artifact: last change at or before the pin; line locator: the blamed commit); 'entry (+audit)' adds the audit raw (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-*) where the figure's only source is an audit raw (computed-new; source-only). A '(cont.)' line is a second primary item-raw row for the same figure; a '(see)' line carries N1's note on the row, the primary item-raw row's own note where the status cell does not already carry it (round 3; cut at 700 characters, whole in the JSON), and related item-raw rows, each with its own status (shown, not used in the pass check).

The block (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:68, AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:70, AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:107, AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:137) and README.md (README.md@bbc9ffe:58) say 103/103. The suite at --ref prints 154/154; the committed site check says results/raw/2026-10-07_m-dispositions_sitecheck.txt@bbc9ffe:10 '154/154 reproduced through valence_core'. The pass condition applied here is Code's reading, not a ruling by the design seat or the PI: the block's preamble (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:3) says that where a referent is wrong, Code names the right one in the raw and proceeds, and Code's preflight raw (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-preflight.md@bbc9ffe:198-199) reads N11 as passing if the suite prints N/N from a fresh clone, with N stated in the raw. That reading is flagged for the design seat and the PI. Because N/N alone would also pass a clone that silently skips checks whose artifact is absent, conjunct (e) is added. Every tracked line that says 103/103 is listed in (3) with its kind (live doc, the block, dated record entry); only the live doc and the block make a present-tense claim about the count. README.md is not edited by this audit; the PI decides its wording, and the wording of the README-NOTE the block's §6 item (3) describes. Lineage of the count, from (2e): at 0dd6f56 (the commit git blame names for README.md@bbc9ffe:58's 103/103, which also wrote RESULTS.md@bbc9ffe:11593) a fresh clone prints 103/103 (exit 0). Of those 103 checks, 100 are at --ref with identical expected, got and cited source; 0 are at --ref with a different remainder; 3 are not at --ref ('S2: grounded panel needs no new extraction'; 'S2: vignette is marked ungrounded'; 'S2: an ungrounded axis cannot decide the reading'), each last changed by e1b5c77 (git log -S), whose subject begins 'S2 adjudications applied: vignette grounded and approved verbatim, persona settled on the ...'. Of --ref's 154 checks, the other 54 are later additions, 8 of them first added by that same commit ('S2: the panel is all three neighbours'; 'S2: two neighbours need no new extraction'; 'S2: the vignette is the one that must be built'; 'S2: vignette is grounded by adjudication'; "S2: construct name of record carries 'Ho-style' retired"; 'S2: persona slot settled on the character reading'; 'S2: an unadjudicated axis cannot decide the reading'; 'S2: the approved vignette DOES decide the reading'). So --ref's 154 checks are not a strict superset of the 103; for README-NOTE item (3), 'what 103/103 covers'. | | | | | | | |

Blame: every line locator's entry commit is `git blame -L n,n --porcelain <rev> -- <file>`, run per line (244 distinct (file, rev, line) entries over the primary and related rows' sources and the note locators); each is cross-checked against one full-file `git blame --porcelain` of its file: 244/244 equal.

## 4. The block's pass check (block :23)

*N1 final:* 4a-4g are the provisional round's pass check over the block's quotations, kept as built. Where they say the note is not on this machine or defer to the --note run, section 9 is that run: 9d is the pass check over the note's own figures, 9e the watch-list per conditional STOP, and 9f the span notes that read the 4g corrections against the note's sentences.

*Pass:* every figure is `reproduced` or `log-only` with a locator. *On fail:* a `not found` figure is cut from the note; a `discrepant` figure is corrected in the note to the reproduced value if the discrepancy is transcription, and is a STOP on that item if it is not. (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:23)

Over the 166 provisional rows (176 primary item-raw rows; a row with two primary rows passes only if both do): primary-row statuses {"reproduced": 124, "discrepant": 16, "log-only": 8, "computed-new": 19, "not found": 3, "other": 3, "mixed": 1, "not run": 2}.

- Rows whose every primary row is reproduced or log-only: 123 of 166; 123 of them give a pinned line locator for every log-only primary row.
- Passing rows that an item raw ties to a wording, scope or precision correction (4g): 29 (L001, L004, L006, L015, L016, L044, L046, L054, L057, L059, L101, L105, L110, L111, L113, L114, L115, L116, L117, L125, L134, L135, L166, L136, L138, L139, L142, L149, L157). The figure passes; the note's sentence that carries it still needs a human read against 4g at the --note run, because a correction without a new numeral is invisible to the --note matcher.
- **The pass condition is not met over the provisional set**: the rows below are not found, discrepant, computed-new, not run, or carry a status outside the vocabulary. Each consequence is the block's (:23), applied to the item raw's own classification; Code does not re-decide any of them. Whether a row binds depends on whether the note carries the figure, which this run cannot read.

- **T** (8 rows): the item raw calls it transcription: block :23 corrects the note to the reproduced value (the item raw's printed value; its correction text where it gives one).
- **NT** (1 row): the item raw says it is not transcription: under block :23 that is a STOP on the item if the note carries the figure; the item raw itself declares no STOP; for the design seat and the PI.
- **NF** (3 rows): the item raw classes it as a design-seat claim or a figure label, not a record figure: a note under block :3, not an (i) discrepancy under :62 (N4's STOPs line, results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N4-e2a.md@09c3a8c:394). N4 does not address block :23. Code's reading, for the design seat (not the item raw's): block :23 reads the note's figures, and a design-seat claim or a figure label is not one, so :23's correct-or-STOP fork is not applied here. For L081 the correction is the block's own (:57: 'the post version is relabeled to the record's definition'); N4's relabel text is in the cause column.
- **SR** (4 rows): the item raw calls it a stale referent (the suite prints 154/154, not 103/103) and declares no STOP; N11's pass on N/N is Code's reading in N11, flagged there for the design seat and the PI. N1's reading of block :23 (not the item raws'): if the note carries 103/103, :23's non-transcription branch would read it as a STOP.
- **Conditional** (L053): the item raw makes the status itself conditional on what the note or figure shows (its status_condition, carried verbatim in the status cell); the T branch applies only on the discrepant side of that condition.
- **Two item raws, two statuses** (L055, L058; table 4f): L055: N3 gives 'discrepant (transcription)' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-robustness.md@09c3a8c:385 (JSON figures[15], id ib-3)), N3c gives 'reproduced, over L51-L63' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-compute.md@09c3a8c:311); L058: N3 gives 'discrepant (transcription)' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-robustness.md@09c3a8c:385 (JSON figures[15], id ib-3)), N3c gives 'reproduced, over L51-L63' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-compute.md@09c3a8c:311). The primary row is N3's: block :44 is part (i-b) of N3, and N3c's own referent split puts (i-b) in the N3 robustness raw (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-compute.md@09c3a8c:12). N3's section 11 confirms the range on L51-L63 and says it needs 'from L51' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-robustness.md@09c3a8c:439) without reconciling its ib-3 status with N3c's. N1 does not choose between them; the design seat decides.

## 7. --note mode

Matching rule (round 2; the round-1 matcher compared values only and is replaced). Every numeral in each file (SVG: the text content of text, tspan, title and desc elements) is split into units: a fraction k/n is one unit; otherwise each numeral, with its sign (+, -, ±, or none; a dash after a letter, digit or bracket is a range or a hyphen, not a sign), its unit kind (percent; percentage points, written 'pp' or 'points'; a multiplier attached as '×' or 'x'; else a plain number) and its decimal places. A ledger row's units are those of its span; a numeral-free row takes the units of its primary item-raw rows' figure labels. A note unit is **matched** only if a row unit has the same unit kind, the same value, a compatible sign (none = +; zero is neutral) and the same integer/decimal form, AND the row's context words (its span and its item-raw figure labels) agree with the words of the note unit's own clause (round 3: bounded by ';', a table bar, a bullet or a sentence end) within 60 characters of the unit: one agreeing word that occurs in fewer than 5 rows' contexts, or two agreeing words of any kind (a lone stage, arm or map name such as 'SFT' or 'DPO' is not enough); the agreeing words are printed. A value hit without such agreement is a **candidate (verify context)**, printed with the row's span and status. When more than one row carries the value, a match is labelled **matched; value shared by N rows (verify which)** and every other row with the value is listed as a candidate (round 3). A unit with no such value hit is **not in ledger**; it lists loose hits (the same digits or proportion with a different unit kind, sign or integer/decimal form: 0.3% against −0.3 or 0.003), rounding hits (half-up, two or more places) and item-raw figure rows carrying the value, as pointers only. Bare integers below 100 are 'weak' and never matched. Units caught by the generic rules (dates, hashes, paths, identifiers, section signs) are listed as excluded. 'Matched' is a pointer for the reader, not a verification: the context test is a word overlap.

Limits. An SVG's plotted positions (point coordinates, bar heights) are geometry, not text, and are not read: F2's plotted (FPR, TPR) points must be checked against their data file (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N2-roc.json for ours; N2vi for the source's) by a human or by the Draftsman seat's own export.

Watch-list. Every unit whose value is one of these is surfaced in its own table, whatever it matches (sign ignored; a decimal key with stated places matches only that many places). Each entry's quotes are asserted to occur in the item-raw line cited.

## 8. Notes for the PI and the design seat (none is a STOP declared by N1)

1. Basis (updated at N1 final). Sections 1-8 are built from the block's quotations of the note; the note the block names (v0.2) was superseded by v0.3 and then by v0.4, which section 9 maps unit by unit. A block-quoted figure the note no longer carries stays in sections 2-5 as a row of the block's ledger; it binds nothing in the note. Section 9 is the ledger of the note itself.
2. Rows whose item raw gives no status in N1's vocabulary are listed in 4d with the item raw's own wording: N13 row 15 (the Q1 bet's date) uses N13's vocabulary (block :93); the N3 CV-scheme row is stated 'as stored' without a status row; the block's 'sub-0.05-cosine' wording has no N17 figure row of its own (N17's note F3 addresses it). N1 does not assign them a status.
3. Discrepant rows carry the item raws' classifications. Some item raws state that a discrepancy is a pass-condition outcome rather than a note figure and that block :23 does not attach (N2 rows 21 and 33, for (ii) and (iv)), or that it is a design-seat claim or a figure label, a note under block :3 rather than an (i) discrepancy under :62 (N4); others give a conditional (N2vi section 12; N3 ib-1's status_condition). Those statements are the item raws' and are shown in the status and cause columns. Where this ledger states what block :23 does with them (the NF and SR branch texts, the 4a conditions), it says it is Code's reading.
4. Record-source entry commits use `git log -1 <pin> -- <path>` for artifacts (the last change at or before the pin, which may be later than the commit that first wrote the figure if the file changed afterwards) and the blamed commit for line locators; first-add commits are listed beside artifacts in section 3.
5. Sources outside the record enter no entry commit; rows resting on them show it in section 3. Over the primary rows (computed from the parsed sources): data/echo/stageC_resid_s*.npz (outside the record (gitignored; local only, 102 files on this machine); rows L060, L062); jlens-qwen36/scripts/e2a_sessions.py (outside the record (gitignored clone); rows L081, L164); archive/PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.pre-patch-v2.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); archive/PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.pre-patch-v3.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); archive/PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.pre-patch-v4.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); PREPRINT-v1_1-draft.md (outside the record (not a tracked path at the pin); rows L071).

Validation (every row; each check has a name, and the rules are in the v0.3 final ledger's 10b and in the script, `nr_validate_row`):

- **note-line.** The unit occurs on the cited line of its file: 1034 of 1034 rows.
- **kind-hash.** The unit -> kind assignment hashes to `d78d13b51aad253642eefbd3798a53b25c7a3fabdb76a2a8e1405f472d773d71` (NR_KIND_SHA256): 545 figures, 489 not-figures.

### 9c. The figures, one row each

'status' is the row's (carried from the item raw or the ledger row; 'pass check' differs only where the ledger row's item raw gives no status in N1's vocabulary, or where a discrepant row is corrected in the note). 'corr.' names a correction recorded in 9i. 'source' is the row's first locator and its source value (cut here; whole in the JSON). 'check' = source-line level (A/B), the status check, and the ledger result. 'entry' = the commit at which the figure entered the record (record sources), and, for a figure whose only carrier is an audit raw, that raw's commit (9g).

### 9d. The block's pass check (block :23) over the note's figures

*Pass:* every figure is `reproduced` or `log-only` with a locator. *On fail:* a `not found` figure is cut from the note; a `discrepant` figure is corrected in the note to the reproduced value if the discrepancy is transcription, and is a STOP on that item if it is not. (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:23)

| pass-check status | note | F1a | F1b | F2 | all |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| reproduced | 190 | 17 | 40 | 35 | 282 |
| log-only | 91 | 0 | 2 | 16 | 109 |
| computed-new | 81 | 2 | 6 | 19 | 108 |
| discrepant | 29 | 2 | 0 | 3 | 34 |
| not found | 9 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 11 |
| outside N1's vocabulary (other, L089) | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |

**The pass condition is NOT met.** 397 of 545 figures pass: 391 reproduced or log-only, each with a locator (385 pass the value check, 6 carry a named exemption: S1#119, S3#11, S6#84, S7#12, S7#15, S9#85), and 6 discrepant rows corrected in the note (S1#184, S1#73, S5#81, S5#38, S5#88, S8#119; for S1#73 and S5#38 the rule in the v0.3 final ledger's 10b; for the four R1 rows, the ruled path nr_check_ruled (branch C below; 10d)). The rest, by the block's branches (each consequence is block :23's, applied to the row's own classification; Code decides none of them):

**Discrepant**, by the branch each row's cause reads into (`NR_DISC`; the quoted words are asserted to occur in the cause). Every discrepant row here carries its item raw's own classification (round 5):

- **T** (7: S1#71 note:3 '.81', S1#72 note:3 '.96', S11#18 F1a:16 '.81', S11#19 F1a:16 '.96', S10#81 F2:52 '0', S10#36 F2:52 '0.003', S10#60 F2:85 '0.003'): transcription (the row's cause), not yet corrected where the row sits: block :23 corrects the note (or the SVG) to the reproduced value; not a STOP. For F2's three W3 units (S10#36, S10#60, S10#81) the PI's ruling R1 of 2026-10-09 names the value: 0.000, and F2's instruct point (0.000, 0.009).
- **C** (6: S1#184 note:3 '0', S1#73 note:3 '0.095', S5#81 note:92 '0.000', S5#38 note:98 '0.095', S5#88 note:98 '0%', S8#119 note:208 '0.000'): corrected in the note: the item raw's discrepancy was a transcription in an earlier text and the note now carries the reproduced value. Either the item raw's correction is quoted from its figure row and the note's figure ties to the source value at level A (corrected_in_note, verified; round 5, finding 6), or (round 8) the item raw states the correction only in a condition and a ruling of the PI settles it: R1 of 2026-10-09 for W3, declared per row in NR_RULED_CORRECTIONS and verified by nr_check_ruled (the condition's quote on its line, the figure row and the condition both cited, the note's value equal to the ruled 0 exactly and different from the record's 0.003 exactly; the source's own clause on the figure row's line, declared per row ('source v1 text 0.000', N2vi :26), stating exactly the ruled 0 and found verbatim, as a whole clause, on that line and in the row's source_value outside its bracketed notes (fix round 3: a bare 0 elsewhere, such as '0 hits' or 'layer 0', no longer meets it; fix round 5: nor a clause inside a nested or unclosed note; fix round 6: the clause followed by the end of its field or a declared terminator, NR_CLAUSE_END_RX; fix round 7: every occurrence outside a bracketed note bound); the ruled 0 in the condition's quote; and level A, which at zero places the record's 0.003 can meet on its own, so it is not relied on alone). The status is carried, not upgraded, and the row counts as passing.
- **TS** (17: S1#177 note:3 '50', S1#178 note:3 '63', S1#179 note:3 '51', S1#180 note:3 '.10', S1#134 note:26 '.94', S1#214 note:26 '.995', S1#215 note:26 '50', S1#216 note:26 '63', S4#4 note:84 '.94', S4#5 note:84 '.995', S4#38 note:84 '50', S4#39 note:84 '63', S4#10 note:85 '.81', S4#11 note:85 '.96', S4#31 note:86 '.10', S4#32 note:86 '.02', S4#33 note:86 '.03'): transcription (the row's cause), and the note now carries the item raw's correction as a change of scope, bound, referent or mask with the figure itself unchanged (the row's cause says so: 'The note now carries the raw's correction'). corrected_in_note verifies value corrections only, so the row is not marked and does not count as passing; whether an applied scope correction counts as passing is the PI's call (9h).
- **NT** (4: S1#188 note:3 '0.8', S1#189 note:3 '2.0%', S6#102 note:111 '0.8', S6#103 note:111 '2.0%'): not transcription (the row's cause): under block :23 a STOP on the item unless the clause is reworded; Code's reading, the PI and the design seat decide. v0.4 rewords :111 to N5's suggested form (0.8–2.0% by arm, sham highest); whether the rewording discharges the STOP is not covered by the 2026-10-09 rulings (9h).

### 9f. Corrections routed to the note, and whether the note applies them (the mappers' span notes)

Every span note, verbatim, by span. 'markers' lists, in order, the upper-case status markers the mapper wrote in the note (NOT APPLIED, PARTLY APPLIED, NOT CARRIED, CHOICE NOT MADE, OPEN, APPLIED, CORRECTED, FIRES, ...; `NR_NOTE_MARK_RE`). It is mechanical: a note can say 'not done' or 'not applied' in lower case and carry no marker, so read the note. 'STOP' marks a note that uses the word, whatever it says about it. The notes are the chains' (each after one adversarial check and a revision), keyed to the final row keys; the v0.3 notes are in the v0.3 rows file (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N1-note-rows.json@ef5c446), referenced, not copied. Round 8's integration annotations follow the note they qualify, marked 'N1 final'; where an annotation supersedes the note (an integration option the script now applies), the annotation holds. Twenty-one notes' text is edited in place, each with an annotation that says so: in fix round 3, S5 note 13's figures from a scratch scan, which no committed file carries, are replaced by a marked passage (Code's D1; ledger 27), and S3 note 11's row key is rewritten to the re-keyed S3#64; in fix round 4, figures that scratch arithmetic derived from committed values and no committed file prints are withdrawn with a marked passage from S4 notes 4, 7, 8 and 13, S7 note 1 and S10 note 7 (Code's D5; ledger 27), each restated from the committed values it rested on; in fix round 5, widths, partial counts and unit conversions that no committed file prints are withdrawn with a marked passage from S2 note 10, S4 note 12, S8 notes 1 and 14 and S9 notes 10, 12 and 23, S10 note 7's clause that only the withdrawn bounds supported is reworded, and S4 note 7 cites N3 :173 for the ten masks (Code's D7, which completes D5); in fix round 6, the bounds and partial counts D7 missed are withdrawn with a marked passage from S2 note 10 and S9 note 11, S4 note 12's restated statement is scoped to the rows it reads, and three fix-round-5 annotations (S2 note 10's, S9 notes 12's and 23's) are edited in place to drop theirs (Code's E1, which completes D7); in fix round 7, the quotient E1 missed is withdrawn with a marked passage from S9 note 13, and S10 notes 2, 15 and 17 and S11 note 4 name their JSON entries and fields (Code's F1); in fix round 8, S9 notes 11 and 12 call N3c's four-decimal prints and the six-decimal MLP minimum by their precision, not 'stored' (Code's G2), and three fix-round-6 annotations (S1 notes 3's and 5's, S8 note 7's) are edited in place to state W5's verdict read at the precision written (Code's G1). Which span notes stay open is the cross-check at the end of 9h.

### 9g. The commit at which the last of the note's figures entered the record (the tag commit)

Rule, per figure (not-found figures enter nothing): a figure mapped to a ledger row takes that row's entry from sections 2-5; a figure mapped to record lines takes, per record locator, the latest blamed commit of its lines, and over its locators the earliest (a fact enters with its first carrier); a figure mapped to an item-raw figure row takes that row's record sources; a computed-new figure (by its own status, its ledger row's or its item-raw row's; or, where no record entry is found, by the row's source value or the item-raw line it cites) enters with the audit raw: the blamed commit of the first audit-raw line it cites; and a figure whose only carrier is an audit raw committed after the audit pin enters with that raw. Over figures, the latest. Each figure's entry and how it was found are in the JSON (`note_rows_final.entries`).

### 9i. Corrections recorded separately from status (round 5)

A correction is what the note's sentence should say, recorded beside the row's carried status, never as a status (the v0.3 final ledger's 10b). 'whose' says whether the reading is an item raw's or N1's own. Kind: wording, precision or scope. Since round 6 the script declares every correction, with its kind and whose reading it is (NR_CORRECTIONS, the v0.3 final ledger's 11b); the rows here are exactly those.

## 10. Values check: mutation self-test, overwrite guard, known limits (this run), and the round's record

Each mutation changes the rows data in memory and the validation runs again; it is caught when a failure no named exemption explains is reported, or when the run stops (a frozen hash, coverage, or an assertion on a row the script names). A mutation that is not caught, or a known limit that is caught, stops the run before anything is written. The families are generated from the rows at every run. At this run: **188 of 188** fixed mutations caught; **190 of 190** family A-E members caught (7 not applicable); 5 known limits.

### 10a. Fixed mutations and known limits

'caught by' is the whole run's first failure (a coverage stop or a frozen hash can come first); 'row checks' lists every row check that fails on the rows the mutation changed, and 'expected' the row check the self-test requires among them where one is declared (a mutation whose declared check does not fire stops the run).

### 10d. What the validator still does not catch

- Integer and word units can still pass at level B on another distinctive value the row states that is on a cited line (49 rows at level B). An integer unit's own number must be stated in the source_value (the unit-stated check), but the cited line need not carry it. The source-values check covers every decimal (one place or more) and every fraction a source_value states; an integer elsewhere in a source_value, neither the unit nor in a fraction, is not checked (self-test L03).
- A word unit ('one', 'two') passes at level A when the word occurs on a cited line, whatever its source_value says (self-test L02); the word can be a coincidence. S7#12 and S7#15 carry a locator-value exemption that says what their lines do carry.
- Status binding. The status check binds a row's status to the cited item-raw lines that give a status and carry a value the row states, to its quoted status_basis (an item-raw line; a single-term clause where its line gives several terms), or to the ledger row it names, which the ledger-bind check ties to the row. The row checks cannot tell a row's own figure line from another figure's line that carries the same value, or the clause that applies from another clause of the same line. So a status changed together with one more field can still pass them: a locator prefix with a reproduced figure row that carries one of the row's values (87 of the 263 rows whose status is computed-new, discrepant, not found or log-only; family G), a ledger_row swap to a row the locator binds (4 of 263; family H), a status_basis moved with the status to another line or clause of a cited item raw (L01, L04), and a 'related row' field cut to its ledger row (L05). Every such change also changes the input of the status freeze (NR_STATUS_SHA256), so the run fails (status-hash) unless the script is changed with it, where it shows in a diff. The freeze does not judge whether a status is right.
- The frozen declarations (NR_KIND_SHA256, NR_CLAUSE_SHA256, NR_STATUS_SHA256, NR_TEXT_SHA256, NR_EXEMPT_FP, NR_CORRECTIONS with its fingerprints, NR_QUOTE_REVIEWED, NR_DISC, the watch rows and the open items' rows) freeze; they do not judge. A row edited together with its declaration passes, and both show in a diff. Whether a unit is a figure, whether a clause is a fair cut of its sentence, and whether a status, a correction or a comment is right stays the mappers' and their checkers' reading.
- Ledger rows (rewritten in round 7). The ledger-span check accepts any named ledger row that carries the value, so a common value (0.000, 0.5) can pass on a wrong row. Since round 7 the ledger-bind check requires the row's first ledger id to be named in its locator, or one of that ledger row's item-raw lines to be cited, and every ledger id the locator names to be in ledger_row; a swap to a ledger row the locator binds passes the row checks (bullet 3). Every ledger_row change, of any status, fails the status freeze.
- Free text is frozen, not judged. NR_TEXT_SHA256 covers every comment, cause, not-figure reason and validation note, every change record (round5, round6, round7, and round 8's n1v4_round2 and round8, their 'why' and 'previous' included), the provenance fields n1v4_chain and v0_3_row, every span note and annotation, and the file's how_built, schema and provenance; NR_CORRECTIONS' fingerprints cover each correction's text and basis. Beyond that, only the quotes the script asserts (NR_DISC's causes, the open items' and the watch-list's span notes) and the quotation scan read it.
- Not-found rows (round 7). A not-found row's locator and source value are in the status freeze, and every file and line they cite must exist (pins); whether the search they describe was complete stays the mappers' reading.
- The quotation scan (round 7) is a heuristic: a quoted span of fifteen words or more within reach of a name in NR_QUOTE_EXT_RE, in the rows file only. A long quotation without quotation marks, or with no such name nearby, is not flagged, and the script's own prose and the rendered ledger are not scanned; the rule on external quotations stays the author's to keep.
- Every check is a consistency check on the mapping; none re-derives a figure.
- Ruled corrections (round 8). NR_RULED_CORRECTIONS declares, per row, the PI's ruling (R1 of 2026-10-09), its condition line and quote, the figure row, the record's discrepant value and the corrected value; nr_check_ruled verifies the row against that declaration and the cited lines (X01-X09, X11-X13, X17, X22-X24, X34-X38, X52-X59, X63-X67): the source's own clause (declared per row, 'source v1 text 0.000') stating exactly the ruled 0, verbatim on the figure row's line and in the row's source_value outside its bracketed note (fix round 3: a bare 0 elsewhere, '0 hits', '(0 places)', 'layer 0', no longer meets it; X11-X13), matched as a whole clause (fix round 4: no digit or decimal appended or prepended, so 'source v1 text 0.0009' or a declared 'source v1 text 0.0' does not meet it; X17; fix round 5: nor a comma and a digit, 'source v1 text 0.000,3', X24; fix round 6, Code's E4 (a): a whitelist, not a list of refused suffixes, X34-X38; fix round 7, Code's F3 and F4: every occurrence of the clause outside a bracketed note must be bound, not one, so a range restated beside the clause fails, X55; an occurrence has no word character, '.', format or combining character just before it, and is followed by the end of its field (a table cell of the figure row's line, the source_value) or by one of the declared terminators ' [', '; ', ', ' or ' (' each followed by a letter (by Unicode category, so not a superscript digit; since fix round 8, Code's G2, an ASCII letter, so not a modifier or other letter that looks like punctuation, ', ʹ0.003' or ', ª0.003', X66, X67), or ')' where a '(' opened outside a bracketed note before the clause in the field (fix round 8: the '(' counted outside notes, X65) and followed by the end of its field, a declared terminator or an ASCII letter (fix round 8: '(source v1 text 0.000)–0.003' and '(source v1 text 0.000)0.003' fail, X63, X64; NR_CLAUSE_END_RX, nr_clause_end_ok, nr_paren_close_ok), so a range or list ('0.000–0.003', '0.000-0.003', '0.000/0.003', '0.000 to 0.003', '0.000, 3', '0.000, .003', '0.000, −0.003', '0.000, ' with a zero-width space and a digit, '0.000)0.003', '0.000; 0.003', '0.000 (0.003)', '0.000, ³', and since fix round 8 '(0.000)–0.003', '(0.000)0.003', '(0.000) 0.003', '(0.000))', a ')' whose '(' is inside a note, ', ʹ0.003' and ', ª0.003') fails; X34-X38, X52-X59, X63-X67; what follows a declared terminator is not read past the letter after ', ', '; ', ' (' or ')', or past a bracketed note, so ' (to 0.003)', '; or 0.003', ', or 0.003', ', x0.003', ' [x] 0.003' or '(0.000)and' after the clause pass (Code's probes of fix rounds 7 and 8, 10e); nor is what precedes an occurrence read past the character before it, so a value before the clause passes ('0.003, source v1 text 0.000', '0.003–source v1 text 0.000', 'not source v1 text 0.000'; Code's probe of fix round 8, 10e): the figure row's line and every R1 row's source_value put the record's 0.003 before the clause by construction ('record A 0.003 / record B 0.003; source v1 text 0.000'), so the prefix side cannot be closed by a rule on what precedes the clause, and it is a declared limit: the whitelist binds the clause's end, not the rest of the field, and in the rows as built each R1 row's clause occurs once and ends at ' [' or at the end of its field), outside every bracketed note (fix round 5: a nested note is part of the note it sits in, and an unclosed '[' is read as a note running to the end of the field, so a clause inside a nested or unclosed note does not meet it; X22, X23; fix round 6: nr_bracket_mask), the ruled 0 in the condition's quote, and level A, which at zero places the record's 0.003 can meet on its own (X08, X09). The ruling itself is the PI's, routed to Code and stated in this script: no committed file carries it, so the script freezes it (a change shows in a diff) and does not judge it.
- Row tags (round 8 fix round, extended in fix rounds 3-8). A row tag on a line of an audit-outcome raw binds only in the strict form 'path@rev:n (Sx#n)' (the line's first table cell is that key; X10). Since fix round 6 (Code's E4 (b)) the rule is a whitelist, and since fix round 7 (Code's F3) it binds through the locator parser: in each of the five fields pins reads (locator, source_value, status_basis's and corrected_in_note's locators, and the correction's basis) that cites an audit-outcome raw, every character is printable ASCII, 0x20-0x7E (fix round 7: ASCII, so a format, combining, look-alike or full-width character fails the field; fix round 8, Code's G2: printable, so a control character such as DEL inside a key fails it too, X60), the escapes of '#' ('\#', '&#', '&num;') are refused (fix round 8, X61, X62), and a row key (S, digits, '#', ':' or '/', digits, in any case or spacing, inside any bracket or none; NR_ROW_KEY_ANY_RX) may appear only as ' (Sx#n)' directly after an NR_LOC_RE match that is a full 'path@rev:n' of an audit-outcome raw with one line number, the key that line's row; a tag after a bare revision, an item abbreviation, a bare continuation, a range or list, or on a path that is not an audit-outcome raw fails (X14, X15, X18, X47, X48), and so does every other occurrence of a key, after a line number or in prose. Not read as a key: one written with another separator ('S3-36', 'S3.36', 'S3 36'), a key in a field that cites no audit-outcome raw, the history records' 'previous' values (forms tested in Code's probe of fix round 7, 10e), and the free-text fields, which pins does not read (comment, cause, not-figure reason, validation note; frozen by NR_TEXT_SHA256): such a field can cite an audit-outcome raw and carry non-ASCII characters (S6#102's and S6#103's causes cite results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N1-claims-ledger-final.md@ef5c446:1744), and a key added to it is caught by the text freeze only. Fix round 6's strict-tag regex read the path differently from the parser, so a tag after '-@rev:n' or on a record path passed it (the round-6 script checker's forms, now X47, X48). Fix rounds 3-5 had refused loose forms one by one; each is still tested: a bare continuation and a range (X14, X15), a comma list, no space and an en-dash range (X18-X20), a bracket, other text in the parenthesis, 'row Sx#n', lower case, a space before '#', an em-dash range, a second tag after a strict one, a comma before the parenthesis and a slash list (X25-X33), and, added in fix round 6, a period, a spaced en dash, a spaced hyphen, a word, 'row', braces and full-width parentheses before or around the key (X39-X45) and a key in prose (X46), and, added in fix round 7, the bare-revision form, a strict-form tag on a record path, a key split by a zero-width space, a Cyrillic 'Ѕ' and a colon for '#' (X47-X51), and, added in fix round 8, DEL inside a key, a Markdown-escaped key in strict-tag position and an HTML-escaped key (X60-X62). In the rows as built, the only row keys in such fields are the three strict tags (S1#166, S1#171, S1#172); S1#172's prose mention of a row was reworded in fix round 6.
- History records (round 8 fix round, extended in fix round 3). The history-schema check (every change entry names its field and its previous value; no field changed twice from one previous value) runs in the final run and in the dev report alike, and a mutation (X16) asserts that it fires at run level. It checks the records' form, not that a 'previous' equals the value it replaced; some 'previous' values carry a marker by declaration in place of a withdrawn clause (S5#59's comment, how_built (4b); the fix-round-4 edits under Code's D5, how_built (4c); the fix-round-5 edits under Code's D7, how_built (4d); the fix-round-6 edits under Code's E1, how_built (4e); the fix-round-7 edits under Code's F1, how_built (4f)). The markers of fix rounds 4 and 5 replace the sentence or parenthetical that carried the figure, so such a marker can cover text the new value keeps (S8#129, S9#40, S9#108 and others); since fix round 7 the markers of fix rounds 6 and 7 replace only the withdrawn figure or clause.
- Span-note cross-check (round 8 fix round 3, extended in fix round 4). The v0_4 profile requires the table (NR_REQUIRE_SPAN_DISPOSITION): a run whose table is absent, or misses a span note, stops at the profile gate, and the cross-check also runs in the validation stage ('span-note-crosscheck'; X21 appends a span note in memory and the self-test asserts the check fires); a 'carried' note must name an item that is not CLOSED. The table's dispositions are Code's declarations; the check binds their form and their cross-references, not whether a note's finding is in substance discharged.
- Branch TS (round 8). A TS row's assertion is its cause's quote ('The note now carries the raw's correction'); the validator does not check mechanically that the note's sentence carries the item raw's scope, bound or referent correction. The chains' span notes (S1, S4) and their checkers read each one against the note.

### 10e. Round 8 (N1 for note v0.4, 2026-10-09): the record

Later rounds append their checks here. Round 8 built the v0.4 rows file and integrated it into this script's v0_4 profile:

- **Carry-over.** The note's v0.3 and v0.4 texts (219 lines each) differ on 32 lines; the 291 rows of v0.3's rows file (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N1-note-rows.json@ef5c446) on the other 187 lines are carried with their keys, note_file renamed. Five carried rows (S1#100, S1#101, S1#112, S7#68, S7#73) changed a comment where a chain found it untrue of v0.4 (each change in the row's 'round8' record, with the previous value). No carried row's status, mapping, locator or source value changed.
- **Ten chains** (C1-C10) mapped every matcher unit on the 32 changed lines and every text node of the three 2026-10-09 SVGs (F1a new as S11; F1b as S9; F2 as S10): a mapper's set, one adversarial check, the mapper's revision. Checker findings: C1 11 (1 substantive), C2 6 (1), C3 6 (1), C4 6 (0), C5 4 (1), C6 5 (1), C7 9 (2), C8 6 (0), C9 4 (0), C10 6 (0): 63 findings, 7 substantive. Each revision accepted its checker's findings and rejected none, and each applied them, except C7's finding 2 (S8#119's corrected_in_note): C7's revision left it open ('Still open' in map_result.json's C7 final summary) and this integration applied it (R1, NR_RULED_CORRECTIONS), which is why C7's revision lists eight applied changes for nine findings. Each chain's harness (this script's nr_validate_row on its rows) reported 0 failing rows and 0 coverage misses (9a's table).
- **The refactor** (the profiles, the dev report, the guard's v0_4 cases): its builder's regression showed the v0_3 profile reproducing round 7's outputs apart from the script's identity lines; an adversarial check (verdict FAIL) raised five substantive findings (the v0.3 final ledger counting as an item raw; empty open items and mutations passing silently; an asymmetric overwrite guard; JSPACE_REPO moving the guard; the routed-set stop as a v0_3 behaviour change) and three minor ones (the guard covering only the ledgers; the v0_4 pin classes; dead dev-report branches). Its fix round fixed six, fixed one in a gated form (NR_REQUIRE_ROUTED_SET: False for v0_3, True for v0_4; whether to set it for v0_3 is the PI's decision) and one in part (a final run refuses every other profile's final ledger; v0_3 keeps its committed default outputs).
- **This integration** (Code): the v0.4 rows file written from the assembled rows (1,034); the PI's ruling R1 applied through a declared, sourced path (NR_RULED_CORRECTIONS; nr_check_ruled) on S1#184, S5#81, S5#88 and S8#119, each given a corrected_in_note with the ruling and S8#119's locator extended to its condition line (nine field edits on four rows, each in the row's 'round8' record); eight span-note annotations where the chains' integration requests are now applied; branch TS for the seventeen applied scope corrections; every v0_4 table filled; the self-test's mutations re-keyed (a mutation whose v0.3 row v0.4 cut or changed in kind names its v0.4 row and says so) and X01-X07 added for the ruled-correction path; the four frozen hashes computed last.
- **The integration's fix round.** Three adversarial checkers read the integrated candidate (rows file sha256 fabfd4c8…, script 4703853a…, ledger 96cdff2e…): row values (verdict FAIL; 8 findings, 2 substantive), the ledger's content against the rulings and the span notes (FAIL; 15 findings, 6 substantive) and script integrity (5 findings, 2 substantive). Every finding was applied; none was rejected. Two were applied in a form other than the one suggested, and one optional part was not taken up, each said below. Row edits are in each row's 'round8' record (finding 'round-8 integration fix round (Code)', with the previous value); the history entries were completed in place.
  - Rows 1 (substantive): S1#172 cited ef5c446:1511 (S7#25) for S7#28, which is :1512; fixed. The optional binding is added: a locator that tags a line of an audit-outcome raw with a row key ('path@rev:n (Sx#n)') must name the row that line holds (nr_check_pins; mutation X10).
  - Rows 2 (substantive) and Ledger 3 (substantive): :98's 'at any n above a single configuration's, the DPO and instruct differences are significant ... between about 1 and 2' goes beyond the n N2 tabulates. Recorded as N1's scope corrections on S1#25 and S5#59 (declared in NR_CORRECTIONS), with O4's residue. S1#208/S1#209's suggested wording is restricted to N2 5(b)'s three tabulated n. (The fix round's first form also cited thresholds from a scratch scan of untabulated n; no committed file carries that scan, and fix round 3 withdraws its figures, Code's D1, below.)
  - Rows 3: S7#78 '32' is kept as an identifier (option b). O8, S1#160's comment and S1 span note 19 (annotated) now say 'reworded; its claim stays not found on S7#25', not 'cut'.
  - Rows 4: the rule is stated in S4#9's and S1#179's comments and in O13: a scope unit shared by a reproduced series and a discrepant series takes the reproduced one's status. O13 names S4#9 as the passing carrier.
  - Rows 5: S3#129's unsourced 'from 2B to 72B' is dropped.
  - Rows 6: the history records.
    - The checker counted seven C2 'n1v4_round2' entries without 'previous'. The six it lists are all there are (a scan of every record finds six), and each is filled with the chain's round-1 value from C2/rows_r1.json.
    - S1#100's and S1#112's second 'comment' change now gives C1's value, which it replaced, as 'previous'. They are kept as two sequential entries rather than merged (applied in another form: the sequence keeps what each change replaced).
    - A history-schema check is added to the v0_4 run: every changed[] entry carries field and previous, and no field is changed twice from one previous value.
  - Rows 7: (a) S1#160 now says four units across three items. (b) O20 drops 'and counts two'; line 13's 'two' (F1 = F1a + F1b, and F2) holds. (c) S1#184's cause names :92 and :98 as the body and :208 as the PI section.

  - D5 (Code's decision; ledger 27, as the brief states it: a figure that only a scratch computation supports may not appear in the rows, the tables or the ledger): D1 named only the n scan's figures, and the checkers found the same class elsewhere. The rule now reads: (a) a figure is admitted when a committed file prints it, or prints the same quantity at higher precision (a rounding is a reading), and the row cites that carrier; (b) a reading of an audited input itself (the note's or an SVG's text, a word count of the sha256-pinned note, an SVG's drawn coordinate converted to data units) is the observation the audit makes of that file, not a figure about the record, and is admitted as such, labelled a scratch read where it is one; (c) a figure that scratch arithmetic, statistics or a recount derives from committed values (a mean, median, ratio, probability, bound, difference, quotient) and that no committed file prints is withdrawn, its committed inputs and the operation named, and the conclusion kept only where it needs no withdrawn figure. The n scan's figures were class (c); so are these. Withdrawn: S10#99's binomial figures (Code's P of zero hits for exploration Instruct and the four greedy arms, and greedy SFT's one- and two-sided bounds), S10 span note 7's same figures and O26's derived P; S4#31's and S4 span note 7's means and medians of N3's printed per-mask medians (its median was also mis-rounded, as the rows checker found); S4 span note 13's ratios; S4#47's and S4 span notes 4 and 8's mean and median of the stored series; S7#79's and S7 span note 1's per-level changes from N19's JSON; and the scratch quotients in the comments of S6#101 and, found by Code's own scan this round, S6#102 and S6#103. Each is restated from committed lines: O26 and S10#99 from N2 :67 and :401-402 (the exploration arms, the only ones a committed raw tests), :348 and N2vi :20 and :22, the test left to the Digest seat or an item raw; S4#31 and S4 note 7 from N3 :160-171 and R1 (:362), as O13 already is; S4#47 and S4 notes 4 and 8 from N3c :85-94 (every per-layer value is in 0.9877-1.0000, so the mean and median round to 1.0 with no derived figure); S4 note 13 from N3 :160-167 and :195; S7#79 and S7 note 1 from N19 :62, :66, :73 and :121 (the bound under weightings on Levels 3-5 is said to be stated by no committed raw). The 'previous' values these edits record carry a marker in place of the withdrawn clause (how_built (4c)); the six notes are edited in place with a marker and each annotated; make_rows.py asserts that no string of the rows file carries the withdrawn figures. Admitted, with the reason recorded: S1 span note 15's and S1#75's/S1#89's word counts (b; S1 note 15 annotated), the geometry reads of F1b and F2 (b), and S3 span note 11's range, which N8 @02b6a61:237 prints (a; annotated). The N4 correction raw prints them: [-0.0621, 0.1289] for 54/101 and [-0.0511, 0.3119] for random1 in its Δ_first column (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N4-correction.md@e60b633:37, :39), so they are class (a) and stay.
